        Idea:
# Sprint 7: Orchestrator Image, Pools, and Artifact Contract

## Overall Context

This sprint makes deployment, pool health, and artifacts canary-ready after the selector contract exists. It spans `reigh-worker-orchestrator/` and `reigh-worker/`.

## Shared Operating Rules

- WGP and VibeComfy remain coinstalled in the worker image.
- Stale workers must not claim newer selected routes.
- Artifact paths, debug retention, redaction, LoRA cache limits, sweeps, and quota alerts must be testable.
- Telemetry must be visible through the chosen transport, not only local logs.

## Sprint Goal

Make orchestrator image, pools, health checks, and artifact lifecycle canary-ready.

## Required Deliverables

- WGP/Comfy startup examples.
- Backend/profile flags.
- Health probes.
- Model/custom-node/template preflight.
- Warm-cache strategy.
- Disk-near-full behavior.
- Drain/kill/restart policy.
- Pool sizing and rollback reserve.
- Artifact path/prefix/TTL/debug retention/redaction/LoRA cache/orphan sweep/quota alert contract.
- Telemetry transport for backend/template/profile/run id.

## Exit Criteria

WGP and Comfy pools launch from the same image; stale workers cannot claim newer routes; artifact cleanup and debug retention are testable; labels are visible in the chosen telemetry transport; staged rollback exercise passes.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Sprint 7 Orchestrator Image, Pools, and Artifact Contract

## Overview
This sprint spans `reigh-worker-orchestrator/` and `reigh-worker/`. The repo already has the right foundations: RunPod startup script rendering in `gpu_orchestrator/runpod/startup_script.py:38`, worker lifecycle/scaling in `gpu_orchestrator/worker_state.py:115`, worker claim payloads in `source/core/db/lifecycle/task_claim_flow.py:24`, route/backend/profile resolution in `source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py:139`, and output/storage helpers in `source/utils/output_paths.py:25`.

The direct implementation should avoid a second image or a parallel orchestration path. Keep WGP and VibeComfy in one worker image, make backend/profile selection explicit at worker startup and claim time, then formalize artifact lifecycle and telemetry through the existing heartbeat/log path.

## Phase 1: Startup Contract and Image Flags

### Step 1: Add backend/profile startup examples (`reigh-worker-orchestrator/env.example`, `reigh-worker-orchestrator/README.md`, `reigh-worker/README.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Document two same-image startup modes: `REIGH_BACKEND=wgp` with `--wgp-profile`, and `REIGH_BACKEND=vibecomfy` with a VibeComfy memory profile.
2. Include example RunPod/orchestrator env values for `RUNPOD_CONTAINER_IMAGE`, selector namespace/version, backend, profile, warm cache, and artifact retention.
3. Keep examples operational, not a broad runbook.

### Step 2: Thread backend/profile flags into startup rendering (`gpu_orchestrator/runpod/startup_script.py:38`, `gpu_orchestrator/runpod/worker_startup.template.sh:421`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Extend `render_startup_script()` to accept `worker_backend`, `selector_namespace`, `selector_version`, `wgp_profile`, and `vibecomfy_memory_profile`.
2. Export those values in the shell template before worker launch.
3. Replace the hard-coded `--wgp-profile 1` in `worker_startup.template.sh` with the configured WGP profile.
4. Add a VibeComfy launch branch only if the existing worker entrypoint can run VibeComfy tasks directly; otherwise keep the same worker process and use `REIGH_BACKEND=vibecomfy` as the selector/dispatch mode.

### Step 3: Make worker metadata advertise its route contract (`gpu_orchestrator/worker_spawner.py`, `gpu_orchestrator/runpod/worker_startup.template.sh`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add worker metadata fields for `backend`, `profile`, `selector_namespace`, `selector_version`, `image`, and `startup_contract_version` during spawn/startup phase updates.
2. Include the same fields in startup logs for postmortem checks.
3. Add tests in `reigh-worker-orchestrator/tests/gpu_orchestrator/runpod/test_startup_script.py` proving rendered WGP and VibeComfy examples use the same image but different env/flags.

## Phase 2: Stale Worker and Pool Safety

### Step 4: Include selector version in claim payloads (`source/core/db/lifecycle/task_claim_flow.py:48`, `source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py:209`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add `selector_version`, `worker_profile`, and `worker_contract_version` to the `claim-next-task` payload next to `worker_backend` and `selector_namespace`.
2. Prefer a single helper in `template_routing.py` for worker selector identity so tests can verify the exact payload.
3. Update `tests/test_task_claim_flow.py` to prove stale workers send old selector versions and therefore can be rejected by the claim service.

### Step 5: Add orchestrator-side stale route health classification (`gpu_orchestrator/worker_state.py:115`, `gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/lifecycle.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Extend derived worker state with a `route_contract_stale` flag when worker metadata selector version/backend/profile no longer matches orchestrator config.
2. Treat stale route workers as drain candidates: finish active work if any, but do not count them as capacity for newly selected routes.
3. Terminate stale idle workers with a structured reason such as `STALE_ROUTE_CONTRACT`.
4. Add focused tests in `reigh-worker-orchestrator/tests/gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/test_lifecycle.py` and `tests/test_scaling_decision_limits.py`.

### Step 6: Add pool sizing and rollback reserve config (`gpu_orchestrator/config.py:15`, `gpu_orchestrator/worker_state.py:412`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add config for per-backend desired reserve, rollback reserve, and max stale drain percentage.
2. Keep the first implementation simple: compute total desired capacity using current scaling logic, then protect a configured reserve for rollback-compatible workers.
3. Log pool counts by backend/profile/selector version through existing scaling decision logs.
4. Cover pure scaling behavior with unit tests before touching RunPod calls.

## Phase 3: Health Probes and Preflight

### Step 7: Implement model/template/custom-node preflight (`reigh-worker/source/runtime/worker/server.py:421`, `reigh-worker/source/runtime/bootstrap_service.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add a lightweight preflight module that checks required imports, WGP path/submodule, VibeComfy availability, template manifest presence, model cache paths, and custom-node manifests.
2. Run preflight before declaring startup phase `ready`.
3. Report preflight results in worker metadata and heartbeat logs.
4. Add tests in `tests/test_worker_postprocess_and_bootstrap_service_contracts.py` or a new focused `tests/test_worker_preflight_contract.py`.

### Step 8: Add health probes for runtime readiness (`source/runtime/worker/local_http.py`, `source/runtime/worker/guardian.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Extend health output to include backend, profile, selector namespace/version, preflight status, disk status, and current run id when available.
2. Send the same labels through heartbeat metadata/logs, not just local logs.
3. Add tests for JSON shape and redaction.

### Step 9: Define warm-cache strategy (`gpu_orchestrator/runpod/worker_startup.template.sh:347`, `source/runtime/worker/server.py:620`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Replace the single hard-coded preload model with a config-driven warm-cache manifest.
2. Support backend/profile-specific warm cache entries while preserving the existing “skip preload when tasks pending” behavior.
3. Emit cache-hit/cache-miss/warmup labels through telemetry.
4. Unit test startup script rendering and worker preload argument construction.

## Phase 4: Artifact Lifecycle Contract

### Step 10: Formalize artifact path and prefix policy (`source/utils/output_paths.py:25`, `api_orchestrator/storage_utils.py:37`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add a small artifact contract module defining canonical prefixes: final outputs, intermediates, debug bundles, thumbnails, and local-only temp files.
2. Route `prepare_output_path()`, `upload_intermediate_file_to_storage()`, and API orchestrator storage utilities through this contract.
3. Preserve existing task-id prefix behavior and collision handling.
4. Extend `tests/test_output_paths_contracts.py`, `tests/test_output_paths_filename_policy.py`, and orchestrator storage tests.

### Step 11: Add TTL, debug retention, and redaction policy (`source/runtime/worker/local_http.py:52`, `source/utils/output_paths.py:130`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Define env-configurable TTLs for final artifacts, intermediate artifacts, debug artifacts, and local materialized files.
2. Keep debug artifacts longer only when debug mode is enabled or the task failed.
3. Add a redaction helper that strips secrets, signed URLs, auth headers, and raw tokens from artifact metadata/log labels.
4. Test redaction with representative URLs and headers.

### Step 12: Add LoRA cache limits and orphan sweep (`source/models/lora/`, `source/runtime/worker/server.py:620`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Find the existing LoRA download/cache code and add size/count/age limits there rather than adding another cache path.
2. Sweep orphaned LoRAs that are not referenced by active tasks or the warm-cache manifest.
3. Emit `lora_cache_bytes`, `lora_cache_evictions`, and sweep result telemetry.
4. Add tests near `tests/test_lora_flow.py` and `tests/test_lora_sanitizer.py`.

### Step 13: Implement disk-near-full behavior and quota alerts (`gpu_orchestrator/worker_spawner.py:185`, `gpu_orchestrator/config.py:55`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Use existing storage thresholds as the source of truth for worker disk state.
2. Add worker-side local disk checks before claiming tasks and before large artifact writes.
3. On near-full, stop claiming new work, send a structured alert, sweep eligible artifacts/LoRAs, and only fail tasks if required space still cannot be recovered.
4. Test pure threshold behavior and claim suppression without requiring RunPod.

## Phase 5: Telemetry and Staged Rollback

### Step 14: Add structured telemetry labels (`source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py:209`, `source/runtime/worker/guardian.py:62`, `orchestrator_common/logging_helpers.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Standardize labels for `backend`, `template_id`, `profile`, `run_id`, `route_key`, `selector_namespace`, and `selector_version`.
2. Attach them to worker logs, heartbeat payloads, task lifecycle anchors, and orchestrator scaling/lifecycle logs.
3. Add tests proving labels appear in the transport payload, not only in local log strings.

### Step 15: Add drain/kill/restart and rollback exercise tests (`gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/lifecycle.py`, `gpu_orchestrator/worker_state.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Encode drain versus kill decisions as pure functions where possible.
2. Add test cases for idle stale worker termination, active stale worker drain, preflight-failed restart, disk-full claim suppression, and rollback reserve preservation.
3. Add a scripted dry-run validation command or test fixture that simulates old selector version workers plus new selector version tasks.

## Execution Order
1. Start with config/env parsing, startup script rendering, and claim payload tests. These are cheap and establish the contract.
2. Add stale route classification and scaling reserve logic next, because pool safety depends on it.
3. Add preflight and health metadata before artifact retention, so artifact/debug behavior can be labeled and tested.
4. Implement artifact path/TTL/redaction/LoRA/disk behavior after the telemetry fields exist.
5. Finish with rollback/drain tests and staged dry-run validation.

## Validation Order
1. Run focused worker tests first: `uv run pytest tests/test_task_claim_flow.py tests/test_template_routing.py tests/test_output_paths_contracts.py` from `reigh-worker/`.
2. Run focused orchestrator tests: `pytest tests/gpu_orchestrator/runpod/test_startup_script.py tests/gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/test_lifecycle.py tests/test_scaling_decision_limits.py` from `reigh-worker-orchestrator/`.
3. Run broader suites only after focused tests pass: `uv run pytest tests` in `reigh-worker/` and `pytest tests` in `reigh-worker-orchestrator/`.
4. For canary rehearsal, run a dry-run rollback scenario with old/new selector versions and verify old workers drain instead of claiming new routes.

  "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-013",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the package is internally inconsistent. the plan body says to keep `activepairdata` in `usestate` and make phase 2 optional, but the attached metadata and success criteria still describe the previous derive-via-`usememo` / remove-`setactivepairdata` / simplify-`onpairclick` plan. that means the approved-plan requirements are only partially coherent as presented.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-108",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the linux distro note is documentation-only; the generated install command in step 7 still runs `apt-get install python3.10-venv python3.10-dev ffmpeg` without any pre-check that the package exists. users on ubuntu 24.04+ who ignore the doc and try to copy-paste the command will hit a confusing `e: unable to locate package python3.10-venv` from apt rather than a targeted error from commandutils.ts. a one-line detection pre-check (e.g., `apt-cache show python3.10-venv >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo 'install deadsnakes ppa first \u2014 see readme'; exit 1; }`) would convert the silent failure into an actionable error, but the plan does not add this.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-145",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the revised v4 body against `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md` sprint 2. the repo plan doc still lists an \"ltx-2 `self_refiner` smoke against the pre-sprint behavioral baseline\" as a sprint 2 verification gate and repeats that smoke in the functionality-preservation checks, but the revised execution steps no longer schedule that smoke anywhere; it survives only as an info-level metadata criterion. because `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` remains an explicit sprint 2 deliverable, the revised plan still only partially carries forward the verification package described in the source migration plan.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-153",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: worker integration: the migration doc describes the per-call profile override gap as involving reigh-worker adapter handling of override_profile before constructing vibecomfy sessionconfig, but the plan limits sprint 1 per-run work to the vibecomfy cli/runtime. that may be acceptable for a vibecomfy-library mvp, but it does not fully exercise the production-facing per-task override semantics called out in the overall context.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-vibecomfy-memory-20260506-0147"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-155",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: a residual issue-hint tension remains for resolution handling. the plan acknowledges current `vibecomfy run` lacks a resolution flag, but allows the adapter to leave resolution and other template-specific values unwired for sprint 2. because `z_image_turbo` and `qwen_image_2512` are marked vibecomfy-supported direct routes, this may not preserve the existing worker task-parameter contract for tasks that provide a non-default `resolution`.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2-adapter-seam-and-20260506-0249"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-014",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: phase 1 still misses a real supporting-data location: `trailingpairdata` lives outside `pairdatabyindex` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts#l111), but the proposed sync logic only considers `pairdatabyindex`. if trailing slot regenerate is part of the bug surface, that location is not covered.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-110",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the non-swallowing helper `update_worker_phase_strict` is a new shell function, but phase 1 step 2 introduces it only as pseudo-code. there is no concrete note that the helper must handle supabase http non-2xx responses as failures (curl returns 0 even on http 4xx/5xx by default unless `-f` or `--fail-with-body` is used). if the implementer ports `update_worker_phase` verbatim with only `|| return 0` removed, a 500 from supabase would still report success because curl's exit code is 0. the plan should specify `curl --fail` or explicit http status code checking in the strict variant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-142",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the supporting-infrastructure review still finds one missing check. in `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md`, sprint 2's verification matrix and risk register both single out `self_refiner` as a distinct drift-upgrade surface with its own smoke and its own silent-behavior-change risk, but the revised v4 body updates the file without keeping that supporting verification step in the actual execution checklist.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-154",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: documentation/data gap: the sprint exit criteria require profiles 1 and 3 to have vram and wall-clock data, but the plan puts that only in metadata as an info-level criterion and does not schedule a concrete artifact location or command for recording the measurements. the implementation could satisfy all unit tests while still missing one of the explicit exit criteria.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-vibecomfy-memory-20260506-0147"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-158",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the supporting infrastructure for resolution is still incomplete if direct vibecomfy routes are considered production-supported. vibecomfy has a `vibecomfy.patches.resolution` helper and the cli can load scratchpad files with `allow_scratchpad=true`, but the plan does not require using that path; it permits leaving resolution out for sprint 2.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2-adapter-seam-and-20260506-0249"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-162",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: checked vibecomfy profile wording against `vibecomfy/tests/test_ready_templates.py`. the revised plan says to add the production template to representative compile profile coverage 'alongside the existing dry-run id', but the dry-run id is not currently in `profile_smoke_template_ids`; only `video/wanvideo_wrapper_22_5b_i2v` and `video/wan_t2v` are. this is a small wording mismatch rather than a functional blocker because adding either the production id alone or both ids would still satisfy sprint 4.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

      ]

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-109",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the step 2 retry loop uses a naked `sleep 2` between attempts, which adds up to 6 seconds of latency on the happy path when the first patch eventually succeeds on attempt 2 or 3. for pods where supabase is reachable but slow during the initial moments of a cold runpod start, the retry logic is fine. however, if the first attempt fails with a tls handshake delay and the retry loop sleeps 2s per attempt regardless of whether curl itself has been blocking for tens of seconds, the total startup latency penalty could be substantial. this is a minor tuning concern, not a correctness issue.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-146",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: finding callers of the changed module shows that `shared.utils.self_refiner` is consumed by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx2_handler.py`, multiple ltx2 pipeline modules, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`. the revised validation steps still only drive the three bridge getters in `source/runtime/wgp_ports/vendor_imports.py`; none of the scheduled commands execute a real `self_refiner` caller, so the plan does not yet verify the main caller shapes for the other explicit sprint 2 file change.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-159",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked direct queue caller values in `db_task_to_generation_task()`: direct image tasks may carry `resolution`, `steps`/`num_inference_steps`, `seed`, and `override_profile`. the revised adapter plan covers seed, steps, and memory profile but not guaranteed resolution propagation, so it does not handle all relevant direct-route caller arguments for a supported vibecomfy direct route.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2-adapter-seam-and-20260506-0249"
      ]
    }
  ],

    {

      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: checked the broader runtime surface after the v4 path fix. sprint 2 is still moving two behavior-sensitive seams rather than just the import-path contract: the `wan2gp/` mount path and the `self_refiner` runtime. step 6.2 covers only the three `vendor_imports` getters, while the 6-file travel suite and the bridge-contract tests do not mention `self_refiner`, so dropping the dedicated self-refiner smoke from the runnable steps leaves the broader downstream surface of that changed file under-covered.",

    },

  {
    "subsystem": "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct",
    "total_occurrences": 7,
    "plan_count": 4,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-007",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-008",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-018",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-106",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-107",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-143",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the path-mapping defect from the previous iteration is fixed, but a different technical gap remains around `self_refiner`. repository search shows `shared.utils.self_refiner` is imported by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/distilled.py`, `ti2vid_one_stage.py`, `ti2vid_two_stages.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/helpers.py`, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`; the v4 plan still aligns `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` to upstream head without any concrete runtime step that exercises those code paths after the change, so the only repo-documented behavior check for that file is still missing from the executable body.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-156",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the direct adapter still has a correctness risk around resolution parity. current vibecomfy ready templates hardcode image dimensions (`image/z_image` uses `emptysd3latentimage` 1024x1024 and `image/qwen_image_2512` uses 768x768), while `vibecomfy run` exposes no `--resolution` flag; the revised plan says a temporary scratchpad/input file may be generated only if supported, otherwise resolution is left out. that means a vibecomfy-supported direct route can silently ignore the worker's `resolution` param unless scratchpad resolution patching is made mandatory for supported direct routes or the support is explicitly limited to default-resolution smoke fixtures.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-2-adapter-seam-and-20260506-0249"
        ]
      }
    ]

    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-013",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the package is internally inconsistent. the plan body says to keep `activepairdata` in `usestate` and make phase 2 optional, but the attached metadata and success criteria still describe the previous derive-via-`usememo` / remove-`setactivepairdata` / simplify-`onpairclick` plan. that means the approved-plan requirements are only partially coherent as presented.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,

        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-108",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the linux distro note is documentation-only; the generated install command in step 7 still runs `apt-get install python3.10-venv python3.10-dev ffmpeg` without any pre-check that the package exists. users on ubuntu 24.04+ who ignore the doc and try to copy-paste the command will hit a confusing `e: unable to locate package python3.10-venv` from apt rather than a targeted error from commandutils.ts. a one-line detection pre-check (e.g., `apt-cache show python3.10-venv >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo 'install deadsnakes ppa first \u2014 see readme'; exit 1; }`) would convert the silent failure into an actionable error, but the plan does not add this.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-145",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the revised v4 body against `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md` sprint 2. the repo plan doc still lists an \"ltx-2 `self_refiner` smoke against the pre-sprint behavioral baseline\" as a sprint 2 verification gate and repeats that smoke in the functionality-preservation checks, but the revised execution steps no longer schedule that smoke anywhere; it survives only as an info-level metadata criterion. because `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` remains an explicit sprint 2 deliverable, the revised plan still only partially carries forward the verification package described in the source migration plan.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
        ]
      },

      {
        "id": "DEBT-109",
        "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the step 2 retry loop uses a naked `sleep 2` between attempts, which adds up to 6 seconds of latency on the happy path when the first patch eventually succeeds on attempt 2 or 3. for pods where supabase is reachable but slow during the initial moments of a cold runpod start, the retry logic is fine. however, if the first attempt fails with a tls handshake delay and the retry loop sleeps 2s per attempt regardless of whether curl itself has been blocking for tens of seconds, the total startup latency penalty could be substantial. this is a minor tuning concern, not a correctness issue.",

- Every check must end with at least one finding. Never leave a `findings` array empty. If you found no issue, add one detailed `flagged: false` finding explaining what you checked and why it appears clear.

I checked the repository and the authoritative service is `reigh-app/supabase/functions/claim-next-task/index.ts`; it currently parses `worker_id`, `run_type`, `worker_pool`, `task_types`, `same_model_only`, `max_task_wait_minutes`, and `debug`, but not `worker_backend`, `worker_profile`, `selector_namespace`, or `selector_version`. The worker-side `_claim_route_guard()` in `reigh-worker/source/core/db/task_claim.py` can detect a backend mismatch only after a task has already been claimed and only from response fields such as `claimed_backend`; it does not validate `selector_version` or `selector_namespace`. As written, the plan's rejection invariant is technically unsupported by the actual claim path.\",\n+          \"flagged\": true\n+        },\n+        {\n+          \"detail\": \"Startup flag rendering: I checked `gpu_orchestrator/runpod/startup_script.py` and `worker_startup.template.sh`; the template currently hard-codes `--wgp-profile 1` and exports no backend/profile/selector variables, so the plan is correct that startup rendering is the right place to add those fields. The plan also preserves the existing same-image path through RunPod env/template rendering rather than inventing a second image flow, which matches the current spawner structure.\",\n+          \"flagged\": false\n+        }\n+      ]\n@@\n       \"id\": \"scope\",\n       \"question\": \"Search for related code that handles the same concept. Is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?\",\n       \"guidance\": \"Look at how the changed function is used across the codebase. For example, `generate-upload-url` uses `storagePaths.taskOutput(userId, taskId, filename)` under the `image_uploads` bucket, while `api_orchestrator/storage_utils.py` still has a direct `task_outputs/{safe_filename}` path. A worker/orchestrator-only artifact module will not by itself make path/prefix/TTL/debug retention testable end to end.\",\n+          \"flagged\": true\n+        },\n+        {\n+          \"detail\": \"Telemetry scope: I checked `routing_telemetry_fields()` and its callers in `task_execution.py` and `task_registry.py`; route/backend/template fields are already emitted into worker log metadata for some routing decisions. If canary review needs labels visible on worker metadata/heartbeat, the plan is missing the supporting SQL/RPC migration and tests.\",\n+          \"flagged\": true\n+        },\n+        {\n+          \"detail\": \"Pool metadata: I checked `gpu_orchestrator/worker_state.py`, `control/phases/lifecycle.py`, and `control/phases/worker_capacity.py`; route staleness classification would need both spawn-time metadata and derived-state capacity filtering.         Idea:
# Sprint 7: Orchestrator Image, Pools, and Artifact Contract

## Overall Context

This sprint makes deployment, pool health, and artifacts canary-ready after the selector contract exists. It spans `reigh-worker-orchestrator/` and `reigh-worker/`.

## Shared Operating Rules

- WGP and VibeComfy remain coinstalled in the worker image.
- Stale workers must not claim newer selected routes.
- Artifact paths, debug retention, redaction, LoRA cache limits, sweeps, and quota alerts must be testable.
- Telemetry must be visible through the chosen transport, not only local logs.

## Sprint Goal

Make orchestrator image, pools, health checks, and artifact lifecycle canary-ready.

## Required Deliverables

- WGP/Comfy startup examples.
- Backend/profile flags.
- Health probes.
- Model/custom-node/template preflight.
- Warm-cache strategy.
- Disk-near-full behavior.
- Drain/kill/restart policy.
- Pool sizing and rollback reserve.
- Artifact path/prefix/TTL/debug retention/redaction/LoRA cache/orphan sweep/quota alert contract.
- Telemetry transport for backend/template/profile/run id.

## Exit Criteria

WGP and Comfy pools launch from the same image; stale workers cannot claim newer routes; artifact cleanup and debug retention are testable; labels are visible in the chosen telemetry transport; staged rollback exercise passes.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Sprint 7 Orchestrator Image, Pools, and Artifact Contract

## Overview
This revision changes the starting point. The prior plan was aimed too much at worker-local payloads and helpers, but the critique shows several sprint invariants are enforced at authoritative cross-repo boundaries: task claiming in `reigh-app/supabase/functions/claim-next-task/index.ts`, object-path minting in `reigh-app/supabase/functions/generate-upload-url/index.ts` and `_shared/storagePaths.ts`, and heartbeat visibility in `reigh-worker-orchestrator/sql/20250115000000_create_system_logs.sql`. Those are not scope creep; they are the root contracts that make stale-route rejection, artifact lifecycle, and telemetry canary-ready.

The implementation still keeps the original scope tight: WGP and VibeComfy remain coinstalled in one worker image, backend/profile selection is config-driven, stale workers are rejected before claim when possible, artifacts use one canonical path/retention contract, and telemetry labels are visible through the selected Supabase heartbeat/system-log transport.

## Phase 1: Contract Audit and Shared Types

### Step 1: Confirm current claim, artifact, startup, and telemetry boundaries (`reigh-app/`, `reigh-worker/`, `reigh-worker-orchestrator/`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Inspect the authoritative claim path: `reigh-app/supabase/functions/claim-next-task/index.ts`, its `claim_next_task_service_role` RPC call, and any SQL migrations defining that RPC.
2. Inspect both worker claim builders: `reigh-worker/source/core/db/task_claim.py::poll_next_task()` and `reigh-worker/source/core/db/lifecycle/task_claim_flow.py::claim_oldest_queued_task()`.
3. Inspect artifact path minting in `reigh-app/supabase/functions/generate-upload-url/index.ts`, `reigh-app/supabase/functions/_shared/storagePaths.ts`, `complete_task` storage handling, `reigh-worker/source/utils/output_paths.py`, and `reigh-worker-orchestrator/api_orchestrator/storage_utils.py`.
4. Inspect startup propagation callers for `render_startup_script()` and `start_worker_process()` across `gpu_orchestrator/worker_spawner.py`, `gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/lifecycle.py`, `gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/worker_capacity.py`, and legacy `gpu_orchestrator/control_loop.py`.
5. Inspect heartbeat/log transport SQL in `reigh-worker-orchestrator/sql/20250115000000_create_system_logs.sql` and `20250202000001_create_rpc_functions_existing.sql` before changing worker guardian payloads.

### Step 2: Define one route/pool/artifact telemetry contract (`reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py`, `reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/config.py`, `reigh-app/supabase/functions/_shared/`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add or reuse a compact route identity shape with `backend`, `profile`, `selector_namespace`, `selector_version`, `route_key`, `template_id`, and `run_id`.
2. Mirror that shape in TypeScript edge-function parsing/validation so worker, orchestrator, and edge claim code agree on field names.
3. Add config parsing for backend/profile pools and selector versions in `gpu_orchestrator/config.py`, keeping defaults compatible with the current WGP behavior.
4. Add unit tests for parsing defaults and explicit WGP/VibeComfy values before wiring callers.

## Phase 2: Startup Propagation and Pool Selection

### Step 3: Thread backend/profile/selector config through all startup callers (`gpu_orchestrator/worker_spawner.py`, `gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/lifecycle.py`, `gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/worker_capacity.py`, `gpu_orchestrator/control_loop.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Extend `WorkerSpawnerAdapter.spawn_worker()` and `WorkerSpawnerAdapter.start_worker_process()` to accept a worker pool contract object, not loose optional parameters.
2. Update every caller that starts a worker process so each spawn chooses the intended backend/profile/selector version rather than relying on defaults.
3. Persist the selected contract into worker metadata during worker record creation and startup-script launch.
4. Add focused tests proving both the direct control phases and legacy control loop pass the selected pool contract through to the startup renderer.

### Step 4: Render same-image WGP and VibeComfy startup examples (`gpu_orchestrator/runpod/startup_script.py`, `gpu_orchestrator/runpod/worker_startup.template.sh`, `env.example`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Extend `render_startup_script()` to export `REIGH_BACKEND`, `REIGH_SELECTOR_NAMESPACE`, `REIGH_SELECTOR_VERSION`, `REIGH_WORKER_PROFILE`, `REIGH_RUN_ID`, and backend-specific profile flags.
2. Replace the hard-coded `--wgp-profile 1` in the startup template with the selected WGP profile.
3. Keep the same worker entrypoint unless repo inspection proves VibeComfy requires a separate command; selection should happen via env/dispatch contract, not by image split.
4. Document WGP and VibeComfy same-image startup examples in `reigh-worker-orchestrator/env.example` and the relevant READMEs.
5. Extend `reigh-worker-orchestrator/tests/gpu_orchestrator/runpod/test_startup_script.py` to prove WGP and VibeComfy render distinct backend/profile flags while using one `RUNPOD_CONTAINER_IMAGE`.

## Phase 3: Server-Side Claim Rejection and Stale Worker Drain

### Step 5: Add route contract fields to both worker claim payload builders (`reigh-worker/source/core/db/task_claim.py`, `reigh-worker/source/core/db/lifecycle/task_claim_flow.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add `worker_backend`, `worker_profile`, `selector_namespace`, `selector_version`, and `worker_contract_version` to `poll_next_task()` and `claim_oldest_queued_task()`.
2. Keep worker-side post-claim guards as a secondary safety net, but do not rely on them for the stale-worker invariant.
3. Update `reigh-worker/tests/test_task_claim_flow.py` and add or extend tests for the runtime `poll_next_task()` path so injected-helper tests cannot pass while production polling omits fields.

### Step 6: Enforce stale-route rejection in the authoritative claim service (`reigh-app/supabase/functions/claim-next-task/index.ts`, claim RPC migration)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Parse and validate backend/profile/selector fields in `claim-next-task/index.ts`.
2. Pass those fields into the service-role RPC or equivalent server-side selection layer.
3. Update the SQL/RPC contract so tasks whose selected route requires a newer selector version or different backend/profile are not claimed by stale workers.
4. Return a non-claim response for mismatch without assigning `worker_id` or moving the task to `In Progress`.
5. Add edge-function and RPC tests proving stale workers are rejected before claim, and current workers can still claim compatible tasks.

### Step 7: Add orchestrator stale-worker drain and rollback reserve (`gpu_orchestrator/worker_state.py`, `gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/lifecycle.py`, `gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/scaling_decision.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Extend derived worker state with route-contract fields and `route_contract_stale`.
2. Exclude stale-route workers from capacity for new selected routes.
3. Drain stale workers with active tasks and terminate stale idle workers with structured reason `STALE_ROUTE_CONTRACT`.
4. Add rollback reserve config that protects a minimum amount of rollback-compatible capacity while new selector-version workers are canaried.
5. Cover pure state/scaling behavior before any RunPod-dependent logic.

## Phase 4: Health, Preflight, and Warm Cache

### Step 8: Add model/custom-node/template preflight (`reigh-worker/source/runtime/worker/server.py`, `reigh-worker/source/runtime/bootstrap_service.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Implement a lightweight preflight module checking WGP path/submodule, VibeComfy availability, required imports, template manifest presence, custom-node manifest presence, and required model/cache paths.
2. Run preflight before startup phase `ready` and before the worker is allowed to claim tasks.
3. Persist preflight status in worker metadata and heartbeat telemetry.
4. Add focused tests in a new `reigh-worker/tests/test_worker_preflight_contract.py` or adjacent bootstrap contract tests.

### Step 9: Add runtime health probes with route labels (`reigh-worker/source/runtime/worker/local_http.py`, `reigh-worker/source/runtime/worker/guardian.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Extend `/health` output and guardian payload construction to include backend, profile, selector namespace/version, preflight status, disk status, and run id.
2. Ensure health labels are redacted and safe for system logs.
3. Test JSON health shape and guardian payload shape without requiring a live worker.

### Step 10: Make warm cache config-driven (`gpu_orchestrator/runpod/worker_startup.template.sh`, `reigh-worker/source/runtime/worker/server.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Replace the single hard-coded preload model with a backend/profile-aware warm-cache manifest or env list.
2. Preserve the current behavior that skips preload when tasks are already pending.
3. Emit warm-cache hit/miss/warmup labels through the shared telemetry contract.
4. Test startup rendering and worker preload argument construction.

## Phase 5: Artifact Lifecycle Across Edge and Workers

### Step 11: Move canonical object paths to the edge storage contract (`reigh-app/supabase/functions/_shared/storagePaths.ts`, `generate-upload-url/index.ts`, `complete_task` flow)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Extend `_shared/storagePaths.ts` to define canonical prefixes for final outputs, intermediates, thumbnails, debug bundles, LoRA cache metadata, and local-only temporary artifacts where applicable.
2. Update `generate-upload-url/index.ts` and complete-task storage handling to mint paths only through that shared helper.
3. Align `reigh-worker-orchestrator/api_orchestrator/storage_utils.py` away from direct `task_outputs/{safe_filename}` path construction and toward the same edge-generated path flow.
4. Add TypeScript tests for `storagePaths` and Python tests for API orchestrator storage behavior.

### Step 12: Add TTL, debug retention, and redaction policy (`reigh-app/supabase/functions/_shared/`, `reigh-worker/source/utils/output_paths.py`, `reigh-worker/source/runtime/worker/local_http.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Define artifact classes and TTL/debug-retention metadata at the path-minting boundary so cleanup can be tested independently of local worker paths.
2. Keep debug bundles longer only for debug mode or failed tasks.
3. Add a shared redaction helper for signed URLs, auth headers, tokens, and secret-like metadata before telemetry or debug artifact writes.
4. Add tests with representative storage URLs, signed URLs, headers, and debug metadata.

### Step 13: Add LoRA cache limits and orphan sweeps (`reigh-worker/source/models/lora/`, artifact contract tests)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Locate the existing LoRA download/cache implementation and add size/count/age limits there rather than introducing a parallel cache.
2. Sweep orphaned LoRAs that are not referenced by active tasks, the warm-cache manifest, or protected rollback/canary cache entries.
3. Emit cache bytes, eviction count, and sweep result labels through the telemetry contract.
4. Add tests near `reigh-worker/tests/test_lora_flow.py`, `test_lora_sanitizer.py`, or a focused cache-contract test.

### Step 14: Add disk-near-full behavior and quota alerts (`gpu_orchestrator/worker_spawner.py`, `gpu_orchestrator/config.py`, `reigh-worker/source/core/db/task_claim.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Use existing orchestrator storage thresholds as the source of truth for worker disk health.
2. Add worker-side local disk checks before claiming tasks and before large artifact writes.
3. On near-full, stop new claims, emit a quota alert through telemetry, sweep eligible artifacts/LoRAs, and only fail work if required space still cannot be recovered.
4. Add deterministic tests for threshold decisions, claim suppression, and alert payloads without requiring RunPod.

## Phase 6: Telemetry Transport and Rollback Exercise

### Step 15: Extend heartbeat/system-log transport where labels must be queryable (`reigh-worker-orchestrator/sql/20250115000000_create_system_logs.sql`, `20250202000001_create_rpc_functions_existing.sql`, `reigh-worker/source/runtime/worker/guardian.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Extend `func_worker_heartbeat_with_logs` or add a compatible RPC/migration so worker heartbeat metadata can carry backend, template, profile, route key, run id, selector namespace, selector version, preflight status, and disk status.
2. Preserve existing VRAM/status/log behavior for older workers.
3. Attach the same labels to individual log-entry metadata for detailed task-level filtering.
4. Add SQL/RPC and Python guardian tests proving labels are visible through the selected Supabase transport, not only local logs.

### Step 16: Add staged rollback/drain validation (`reigh-worker-orchestrator/tests/`, `reigh-worker/tests/`, `reigh-app/supabase/functions/claim-next-task/` tests)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add a dry-run rollback test fixture with old selector-version workers, new selector-version tasks, compatible rollback-reserve workers, and mixed active/idle states.
2. Verify stale workers cannot claim newer routes at the edge claim boundary.
3. Verify orchestrator drains stale active workers, terminates stale idle workers, and preserves rollback reserve capacity.
4. Verify artifact cleanup/debug retention and telemetry labels remain testable during the rollback scenario.

## Execution Order
1. Start with Step 1 and Step 2 so every repo agrees on field names before wiring behavior.
2. Land startup propagation across all callers before adding pool behavior, otherwise backend/profile flags risk staying default-only.
3. Implement worker claim payload fields and server-side edge/RPC rejection together; do not treat worker-side post-claim guards as sufficient for stale-worker prevention.
4. Add orchestrator stale-drain and rollback reserve after server-side claim rejection is testable.
5. Add preflight, health, and warm-cache behavior before artifact retention so cleanup and debug behavior can be labeled.
6. Implement artifact path/TTL/redaction in edge storage helpers first, then align worker and API orchestrator helpers to that contract.
7. Finish with heartbeat SQL/RPC telemetry support and the staged rollback exercise.

## Validation Order
1. Run cheap focused worker tests first: claim payload builders, routing contract, startup/preflight helpers, output path helpers.
2. Run focused edge-function and SQL/RPC tests for `claim-next-task`, storage paths, upload URL minting, and heartbeat label persistence.
3. Run focused orchestrator tests for startup rendering/callers, stale state derivation, scaling reserve, drain/terminate policy, and storage threshold behavior.
4. Run broader suites in each touched repo after focused checks pass: `reigh-worker`, `reigh-worker-orchestrator`, and relevant `reigh-app` Supabase function tests.
5. Run the staged rollback dry-run fixture as the final automated gate before any live RunPod canary.

  {
    "id": "issue_hints",
    "concern": "Did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: Claim contract: The plan metadata explicitly asks where the authoritative `claim-next-task` edge function lives, but the execution body keeps claim rejection work inside `reigh-worker/source/core/db/lifecycle/task_claim_flow.py` and worker tests. I checked the repository and the authoritative service is `reigh-app/supabase/functions/claim-next-task/index.ts`; it currently parses `worker_id`, `run_type`, `worker_pool`, `task_types`, `same_model_only`, `max_task_wait_minutes`, and `debug`, but not `worker_backend`, `worker_profile`, `selector_namespace`, or `selector_version`. The plan therefore only partially covers the stale-worker requirement unless it adds the edge function/RPC change or explicitly defers server-side rejection.",
    "status": "addressed",
    "severity": "significant"
  },
  {
    "id": "correctness",
    "concern": "Are the proposed changes technically correct?: Stale claim rejection: Step 4 says tests should prove stale workers send old selector versions and are rejected by the claim service, but the current service-role path in `reigh-app/supabase/functions/claim-next-task/index.ts` forwards only `p_worker_id`, `p_include_active`, `p_run_type`, `p_same_model_only`, `p_max_task_wait_minutes`, `p_worker_pool`, and `p_task_types` into `claim_next_task_service_role`. The worker-side `_claim_route_guard()` in `reigh-worker/source/core/db/task_claim.py` can detect a backend mismatch only after a task has already been claimed and only from response fields such as `claimed_backend`; it does not validate `selector_version` or `selector_namespace`. As written, the plan's rejection invariant is technically unsupported by the actual claim path.",
    "status": "addressed",
    "severity": "significant"
  },
  {
    "id": "scope",
    "concern": "Search for related code that handles the same concept. Is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: Artifact lifecycle: The plan scopes canonical prefixes mainly to `reigh-worker/source/utils/output_paths.py` and `reigh-worker-orchestrator/api_orchestrator/storage_utils.py`, but the actual upload contract also lives in `reigh-app/supabase/functions/generate-upload-url/index.ts`, `reigh-app/supabase/functions/_shared/storagePaths.ts`, and the `complete_task` edge storage flow. For example, `generate-upload-url` uses `storagePaths.taskOutput(userId, taskId, filename)` under the `image_uploads` bucket, while `api_orchestrator/storage_utils.py` still has a direct `task_outputs/{safe_filename}` path. A worker/orchestrator-only artifact module will not by itself make path/prefix/TTL/debug retention testable end to end.",
    "status": "addressed",
    "severity": "significant"
  },
  {
    "id": "all_locations",
    "concern": "Does the change touch all locations AND supporting infrastructure?: Telemetry transport: The plan names `source/runtime/worker/guardian.py` and `orchestrator_common/logging_helpers.py`, but the heartbeat transport is also defined by SQL RPCs in `reigh-worker-orchestrator/sql/20250115000000_create_system_logs.sql` and `20250202000001_create_rpc_functions_existing.sql`. `func_worker_heartbeat_with_logs` currently accepts VRAM, logs, status, and current task id; arbitrary backend/profile/selector labels can appear only if they are attached to individual log entries' metadata, not as worker heartbeat metadata. If canary review needs labels visible on worker metadata/heartbeat, the plan is missing the supporting SQL/RPC migration and tests.",
    "status": "addressed",
    "severity": "significant"
  },
  {
    "id": "callers-1",
    "concern": "Find the callers of the changed function. What arguments do they actually pass? Does the fix handle all of them?: Startup callers: `render_startup_script()` is called from `WorkerSpawnerAdapter.spawn_worker()`, `WorkerSpawnerAdapter.start_worker_process()`, and startup-script tests; `start_worker_process()` is in turn called from `control/phases/lifecycle.py`, `control/phases/worker_capacity.py`, and legacy `control_loop.py` with only `runpod_id`, `worker_id`, and `has_pending_tasks`. The plan says to extend `render_startup_script()` with backend/profile/selector arguments but does not explicitly update the upstream control-loop call sites or config selection logic that would decide which pool gets which backend/profile. Without that propagation, the new parameters risk being default-only and never varying per pool.",
    "status": "addressed",
    "severity": "significant"
  },
  {
    "id": "callers-2",
    "concern": "Find the callers of the changed function. What arguments do they actually pass? Does the fix handle all of them?: Claim payload callers: There are two claim payload builders, `reigh-worker/source/core/db/task_claim.py::poll_next_task()` and `reigh-worker/source/core/db/lifecycle/task_claim_flow.py::claim_oldest_queued_task()`. The plan names the lifecycle helper but does not name `poll_next_task()`, which is the runtime polling path that already sends `worker_backend` and `selector_namespace`; adding `selector_version` and `worker_profile` must be done in both locations to avoid tests passing against the injected helper while the actual worker still omits the new fields.",
    "status": "addressed",
    "severity": "significant"
  }
]

  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [

      "id": "DEBT-007",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",

      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-018",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-106",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-107",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-143",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the path-mapping defect from the previous iteration is fixed, but a different technical gap remains around `self_refiner`. repository search shows `shared.utils.self_refiner` is imported by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/distilled.py`, `ti2vid_one_stage.py`, `ti2vid_two_stages.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/helpers.py`, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`; the v4 plan still aligns `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` to upstream head without any concrete runtime step that exercises those code paths after the change, so the only repo-documented behavior check for that file is still missing from the executable body.",

      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-156",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the direct adapter still has a correctness risk around resolution parity. current vibecomfy ready templates hardcode image dimensions (`image/z_image` uses `emptysd3latentimage` 1024x1024 and `image/qwen_image_2512` uses 768x768), while `vibecomfy run` exposes no `--resolution` flag; the revised plan says a temporary scratchpad/input file may be generated only if supported, otherwise resolution is left out. that means a vibecomfy-supported direct route can silently ignore the worker's `resolution` param unless scratchpad resolution patching is made mandatory for supported direct routes or the support is explicitly limited to default-resolution smoke fixtures.",

      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the non-swallowing helper `update_worker_phase_strict` is a new shell function, but phase 1 step 2 introduces it only as pseudo-code. there is no concrete note that the helper must handle supabase http non-2xx responses as failures (curl returns 0 even on http 4xx/5xx by default unless `-f` or `--fail-with-body` is used). if the implementer ports `update_worker_phase` verbatim with only `|| return 0` removed, a 500 from supabase would still report success because curl's exit code is 0. the plan should specify `curl --fail` or explicit http status code checking in the strict variant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-142",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the supporting-infrastructure review still finds one missing check. in `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md`, sprint 2's verification matrix and risk register both single out `self_refiner` as a distinct drift-upgrade surface with its own smoke and its own silent-behavior-change risk, but the revised v4 body updates the file without keeping that supporting verification step in the actual execution checklist.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-154",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: documentation/data gap: the sprint exit criteria require profiles 1 and 3 to have vram and wall-clock data, but the plan puts that only in metadata as an info-level criterion and does not schedule a concrete artifact location or command for recording the measurements. the implementation could satisfy all unit tests while still missing one of the explicit exit criteria.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-vibecomfy-memory-20260506-0147"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-158",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the supporting infrastructure for resolution is still incomplete if direct vibecomfy routes are considered production-supported. vibecomfy has a `vibecomfy.patches.resolution` helper and the cli can load scratchpad files with `allow_scratchpad=true`, but the plan does not require using that path; it permits leaving resolution out for sprint 2.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2-adapter-seam-and-20260506-0249"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-162",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: checked vibecomfy profile wording against `vibecomfy/tests/test_ready_templates.py`. the revised plan says to add the production template to representative compile profile coverage 'alongside the existing dry-run id', but the dry-run id is not currently in `profile_smoke_template_ids`; only `video/wanvideo_wrapper_22_5b_i2v` and `video/wan_t2v` are. this is a small wording mismatch rather than a functional blocker because adding either the production id alone or both ids would still satisfy sprint 4.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-4-wan-single-frame-and-20260506-0818"
      ]
    }

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-109",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the step 2 retry loop uses a naked `sleep 2` between attempts, which adds up to 6 seconds of latency on the happy path when the first patch eventually succeeds on attempt 2 or 3. for pods where supabase is reachable but slow during the initial moments of a cold runpod start, the retry logic is fine. however, if the first attempt fails with a tls handshake delay and the retry loop sleeps 2s per attempt regardless of whether curl itself has been blocking for tens of seconds, the total startup latency penalty could be substantial. this is a minor tuning concern, not a correctness issue.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-146",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: finding callers of the changed module shows that `shared.utils.self_refiner` is consumed by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx2_handler.py`, multiple ltx2 pipeline modules, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`. the revised validation steps still only drive the three bridge getters in `source/runtime/wgp_ports/vendor_imports.py`; none of the scheduled commands execute a real `self_refiner` caller, so the plan does not yet verify the main caller shapes for the other explicit sprint 2 file change.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-159",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked direct queue caller values in `db_task_to_generation_task()`: direct image tasks may carry `resolution`, `steps`/`num_inference_steps`, `seed`, and `override_profile`. the revised adapter plan covers seed, steps, and memory profile but not guaranteed resolution propagation, so it does not handle all relevant direct-route caller arguments for a supported vibecomfy direct route.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2-adapter-seam-and-20260506-0249"
      ]
    }
  ],

  {
    "subsystem": "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct",
    "total_occurrences": 7,
    "plan_count": 4,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-007",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-008",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-018",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-106",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-107",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-143",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the path-mapping defect from the previous iteration is fixed, but a different technical gap remains around `self_refiner`. repository search shows `shared.utils.self_refiner` is imported by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/distilled.py`, `ti2vid_one_stage.py`, `ti2vid_two_stages.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/helpers.py`, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`; the v4 plan still aligns `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` to upstream head without any concrete runtime step that exercises those code paths after the change, so the only repo-documented behavior check for that file is still missing from the executable body.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-156",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the direct adapter still has a correctness risk around resolution parity. current vibecomfy ready templates hardcode image dimensions (`image/z_image` uses `emptysd3latentimage` 1024x1024 and `image/qwen_image_2512` uses 768x768), while `vibecomfy run` exposes no `--resolution` flag; the revised plan says a temporary scratchpad/input file may be generated only if supported, otherwise resolution is left out. that means a vibecomfy-supported direct route can silently ignore the worker's `resolution` param unless scratchpad resolution patching is made mandatory for supported direct routes or the support is explicitly limited to default-resolution smoke fixtures.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-2-adapter-seam-and-20260506-0249"
        ]
      }
    ]
  },

  {
    "subsystem": "does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure",
    "total_occurrences": 6,

    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-014",
        "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: phase 1 still misses a real supporting-data location: `trailingpairdata` lives outside `pairdatabyindex` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts#l111), but the proposed sync logic only considers `pairdatabyindex`. if trailing slot regenerate is part of the bug surface, that location is not covered.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-110",
        "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the non-swallowing helper `update_worker_phase_strict` is a new shell function, but phase 1 step 2 introduces it only as pseudo-code. there is no concrete note that the helper must handle supabase http non-2xx responses as failures (curl returns 0 even on http 4xx/5xx by default unless `-f` or `--fail-with-body` is used). if the implementer ports `update_worker_phase` verbatim with only `|| return 0` removed, a 500 from supabase would still report success because curl's exit code is 0. the plan should specify `curl --fail` or explicit http status code checking in the strict variant.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]

        "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: documentation/data gap: the sprint exit criteria require profiles 1 and 3 to have vram and wall-clock data, but the plan puts that only in metadata as an info-level criterion and does not schedule a concrete artifact location or command for recording the measurements. the implementation could satisfy all unit tests while still missing one of the explicit exit criteria.",

  {

    "total_occurrences": 5,

    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-013",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the package is internally inconsistent. the plan body says to keep `activepairdata` in `usestate` and make phase 2 optional, but the attached metadata and success criteria still describe the previous derive-via-`usememo` / remove-`setactivepairdata` / simplify-`onpairclick` plan. that means the approved-plan requirements are only partially coherent as presented.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
